| `TestProfileTabsChain_*` (real `Build` chain, fresh reader) | GetUserTimeline media and reposts **24** at page 20, **54** at page 50, 20 warm, 0 on a rejected token, 1 (interceptor) on a flag refusal |
| `TestListLikedPostsChain_*` | **23** page 20, **53** page 50, 5 for the 5 rows of a warm second page, 2 for an empty array, 10 with dropped rows, 0 on a bad token |
| `TestListSuggestedUsers_*` and the chain test | friends of friends **42** (41 in the service + interceptor), warm 0 (+ interceptor 1; with the 60 s snapshot expired, 1 + the interceptor, W3-22 L-1), curated 6 (+ 1) in the 8-id test world |
| `TestW311_GetProfileRelationship_*` | **3** nothing cached, **2** target profile cached, **1** after the header's GetProfile, **0** warm, 1 for a non-follower's first edge read, 3 on an overflowed caller, 1 for a target that blocked the caller |
| `TestUpdateProfile_Integration_ExtrasBudget` | banner + location + website + pin **3 reads / 1 write**; bio only 1 / 1; clear all 1 / 1; flag off 0 / 0; an identical save (W3-22 L-4) within 3 reads / exactly **0 writes** with a banner and a pin, within 1 / 0 for text only |
| `TestIntegration_BannerBudgetsAndResolver` | CreateUpload BANNER **2 reads / 3 writes**; FinalizeUpload BANNER cold **2 reads / 3 writes** (2 writes in the k6-era build; the M-3 claim added one, re-run at `16524df`) and 2 Vision units; ResolveBanner 1 read |
| `TestBackfillHasMedia_*` | reads = posts scanned, writes = posts lacking the key (667 of 1,001 in the production-page-size test), a rerun 0, at most 5,000 writes per run |
| throwaway overlay test of `identity.PinJobs` through the real posts and identity repos (not in the repository) | deleted post that is not the pin **2 reads / 0 writes**; the pin **2 / 1**; redelivery 2 / 0; post still exists (refused) **1 / 0** |

### What was not measured
- The #350 and #351 behaviour under k6 load: the M-3 single flight and claim (asserted with fakes in `finalize_claim_test.go` and with the claim write in the integration test above), the `profile_update_daily` cap (`apiserver/guard_test.go`) and the L-1 snapshot reload are asserted by the merged tests, not load-tested here.
- Cloud Run CPU and memory for the new RPCs and cold-start time (emulators only; the Cloud Run ms column is an estimate), and the instance cache hit rate (no traffic): the call mix of §1 (95% cold suggestions, 85/15 fof/curated) is an input.
- A cold-start wave, a notification fan-out burst and a post receiving 50 likes/s: not Part A shapes (Part A adds no write fan-out and no counter); the shapes that are, a viral profile on the Media tab and a user following 600 accounts, are above.
- The composite indexes (`posts` hasMedia and kind, W3-2) are not enforced by the emulator: the dev smoke after W3-24 must show them READY, and the first real `fs_reads` per tab call comes from there.
- `GetPost` of a pinned post and `FinalizeUpload` through Vision in the chain (existing RPCs; the W3-24 dev smoke is a handful of real calls).

## 13. W3-37 measurements (2026-10-28, emulators and the repository's own budget tests only; nothing was run against a cloud project)
Machine: Windows 11, 16 logical processors, Go 1.27.0 toolchain building the API from `main` 15975e53 (every backend ticket W3-25a to W3-32 merged, including W3-26b, W3-28a to c and W3-29b; the W3-34 client is not), k6 2.2.0, firebase-tools with Firestore, Auth, Pub/Sub and Storage on their own port block (Firestore 34080, Auth 34099, Pub/Sub 34085, Storage 34199, hub 34400, API 34081), through a temporary `--config` that was deleted before the commit and behind `scripts/with-private-tmp.sh`; no other session's emulator was touched. Emulator latency is indicative only.

Scenario `loadtest/private_accounts_w3b.js` (new; the header documents every phase and knob) and `node loadtest/analyze_w3_part_b.js api.log` (new; reads per call position, the Follow `txn_attempts`, the refresh lines). One local API process, so one "instance"; a fresh process for the run (cold caches), `CACHE_TTL` 60 s. API environment: `ENV=local FEATURE_POSTS=on FEATURE_GRAPH=on FEATURE_PRIVATE_ACCOUNTS=on FEATURE_ENGAGEMENT=on CACHE_TTL=60s RATE_LIMIT_PER_IP_PER_MIN=1000000 RATE_LIMIT_PRE_AUTH_IP_PER_MIN=1000000 RATE_LIMIT_PER_USER_PER_MIN=100000 RATE_LIMIT_GRAPH_FOLLOW_PER_MIN=100000 RATE_LIMIT_GRAPH_LIST_PER_MIN=100000 RATE_LIMIT_FOLLOW_REQUESTS_RESPOND_PER_MIN=100000 RATE_LIMIT_USER_TIMELINE_PER_MIN=100000 RATE_LIMIT_POST_CREATE_PER_MIN=100000 LIST_CALLS_PER_DAY=100000 GRAPH_MUTATIONS_PER_DAY=100000 QUOTA_FOLLOWS_PER_DAY=100000 QUOTA_NEW_ACCOUNT_FOLLOWS_PER_DAY=100000 READ_BUDGET_PER_UID_PER_DAY=10000000`. The Part B limits of W3-25b (`QUOTA_PRIVACY_TOGGLES_PER_DAY` 4, `PROTECTED_AUTHORS_MAX`, `RATE_LIMIT_FOLLOW_REQUESTS_RESPOND_PER_MIN`) were left at the defaults except the respond limit. Notifications were off (the fan-out is the ADR-0017 row, not re-measured). Run: 560 requesters, 2 protected owners (500 requests to P, 60 to P2), 20 strangers, 50 simultaneous strangers, 150 s of 6 stranger calls/s; k6 checks 1,674 of 1,674 green; 0 ERROR lines, 0 `graph_txn_contention`, 0 `protected_set_stale`, 0 `protected_set_unavailable`.

| Case | calls | reads / writes / deletes per call | Planning (ADR-0025) |
|---|---|---|---|
| UpdateProfile `is_private=true`, P (cold) then P2 | 2 | **4 / 3 / 0**, then **3 / 3 / 0** | 4 cold / 3 warm, 3 writes |
| **Follow burst**: 560 Follows by 40 virtual users in 0.6 s, 500 to P and 60 to P2 | 560 | **3 / 3 / 0** in 560 of 560; `follow_outcome=requested`; **`txn_attempts` 1 in 560 of 560**; server p50 37 ms, p95 51 ms | 4 cold / 3 warm, 3 writes |
| GetMe of protected P, then again | 2 | **3**, then **0** (count cached 30 s) | +1 read per 1,000 matches, cached 30 s |
| ListFollowRequests page 20, P (500 pending), first call | 1 | **41** / 0 / 0 (the GetMe had cached P's profile) | 42 cold |
| the same page again | 1 | **20** | 21 warm |
| page 2 and page 3 by token | 2 | **40** and **40** | (42 first page) |
| ListFollowRequests page 50, P2 (60 pending), cold, then again | 2 | **102**, then **50** | 102 cold / 51 warm |
| **Respond burst**: 150 requesters on 10 virtual users (100 accepts, 50 declines), none of them on a listed page | 150 | accept **3** (51) or **4** (49) reads, mean 3.49, **4 writes, 1 delete**; decline **2** (30) or **3** (20), mean 2.40, **1 write, 1 delete**; blend 3.13; `txn_attempts` 1 in 156 of 156; server p50 19 ms, p95 30 ms | 3 cold / 2 warm after a List; up to 5 standalone cold (accepted) |
| Respond replay (same idempotency key) | 6 | **1** (4) or **2** (2) / **0 writes / 0 deletes** | replay 2 / 1, 0 writes |
| **Stranger refusal, 50 simultaneous first calls, then 6 calls/s for 150 s**: GetPost of P's post (404) and GetUserTimeline of P (200, `author_protected`) | 481 + 469 | GetPost mean **0.12** reads (**0** on 455 of 481, 2 on 23, 3 on 2, **4** on 1); GetUserTimeline mean **0.16** (0 on 433 of 469, 1 on 1, 2 on 32, **3** on 3); 0 writes | GetPost 4 cold / 0 warm; GetUserTimeline 3 cold / 0 warm, no page read |
| **Protected-set refresh** in the same run | 4 lines | **one** refresh for the 50 simultaneous callers (the singleflight check), then at **+265.2 s** and **+325.4 s**; gaps **60.1 s** and **60.2 s** = `CACHE_TTL`; each line carries `protected_set_reads=1` and its own `fs_reads` equals its neighbours' (2 or 4, 3) | at most 1 per instance per 60 s |

- **The reads of the stranger calls are the per-stranger caches, not a per-call cost.** 20 strangers each re-read their own interceptor profile and graph once per 60 s (2 to 3 reads), so 94% of calls read 0 and the mean is 0.12 to 0.16; the cold maxima are the budgets (4 and 3). The refusal never reads the post or the author's page.
- **Follow to one protected target does not contend.** 500 Follows to P in 0.6 s, 1 attempt each; the ADR trigger (`txn_attempts` above 3, or `ABORTED`) is far away. A real viral protected account is 0.05 of the Follows per DAU and a human rate; this is the worst-case shape.
- **Respond is the one line above its planning typical in the burst (3.13 against 3.0), and it is the standalone shape the founder accepted** ("up to 5 cold"); the maximum seen was 4, and an accept after a List reads 2 (below). **Follow-up for the proto owner (not edited here):** the `RespondToFollowRequest` comment in `graph.proto` still says "reads 3 cold / 2 warm" and does not mention the accepted standalone bound of 5, so a standalone accept on a warm caller (3 to 4) reads above the proto's documented worst case; ADR-0025 "Implementation note (W3-28a)" covers it, so this is a comment-only follow-up and not a FAIL.
- Setup: `CreatePost` by P read 2 / wrote 4 (the existing row) and carried the first refresh (`protected_set_reads=1`).

### Budget tests re-run on the same emulators (`go test -tags=integration -v`, the repository's `budgettest` lines; 25 top-level Part B tests across `apiserver`, `graph` and `identity` and their 47 subtests PASS, 0 FAIL)
| Test | Measured (reads / writes / deletes) |
|---|---|
| `TestPrivacyToggle_Integration_*` and `TestPrivacyToggle_Chain` (W3-26a) | protect cold **4 / 3 / 0**; same value again **1 / 0 / 0**; unprotect with the flag off **3 / 3 / 0**; refused by the daily quota **3 / 0 / 0**; refused at the cap **3 / 0 / 0** |
| `TestW326b_*` (W3-26b) | GetProfile of P by an approved or unapproved viewer **2**, **3**, **3**, **1**; GetMe public caller cold **2**, protected caller cold **3** (+1 count), second call **0** (cache hit true), after a new request **2** |
| `TestW328a_*` (W3-28a) | ListFollowers and ListFollowing of P by an unapproved viewer **3 / 0 / 0** (each viewer kind, 0 page reads); ListFollowRequests cold with 2 rows **6**; Respond accept after a List **2 / 4 / 1**; replay warm **1 / 0 / 0**; replay cold **3 / 0 / 0**; decline **2 / 1 / 1**; flag-off refusals: List **1**, Respond **0** |
| `TestW328c_*` (W3-28c) | the six lists keep their ceilings with `is_private` filled (ListFollowing 8, ListFollowers 6, ListFollowRequests 6, ListSuggestedUsers 9, ListBlockedUsers 4, ListMutedUsers 4 in the test world; +0 reads, 0 writes) |
| `TestW329a_*` (W3-29a) | stranger GetPost cold **4**, follower **5** (4 + the existing `userLikes` read), the quoter's own copy of P's post **4**; LikePost, AddBookmark, ReportContent, reply and quote of a protected post by a stranger **0 reads / 0 writes** after the chain is warm; RepostPost by an approved follower **2 / 0 / 0** with PROTECTED_ACCOUNT |
| `TestW329b_*` (W3-29b) | GetThread NOT_FOUND **3**, a thread **11**, ListBookmarks **13**, ListLikedPosts **8** (small world; ceilings unchanged) |
| `TestW330_*` (W3-30) | GetUserTimeline of P by an unapproved viewer **3** on every tab and with an old `since_token`; an approved follower or the author **3 to 7** (ceilings unchanged); Home with a repost of P dropped **6** |
| `TestPrivacyPublic_Integration_Counts` (W3-28b, `privacy_public` job) | 0 pending **2 / 0 / 0**; 1 **3 / 4 / 1**; 100 **203 / 301 / 100** (2 pages); 101 **204 / 305 / 101**; 130 **262 / 392 / 130**; 250 **503 / 753 / 250** (3 pages). About 2 reads, 3 writes, 1 delete per pending request, +1 write per non-empty page and 1 to 3 fixed reads. **500 pending extrapolates to about 1,006 / 1,505 / 500** (plan 1,000 / 1,505 / 500; the 6 are the per-page reads of ADR note W3-28b) |

### What was not measured
- **The `privacy_public` job under Pub/Sub push, the notification fan-out (`follow_request`, `follow_accepted`) and the eraser and export** at volume: the local API has no push wiring, and the fan-out is an ADR-0017 row. The job is the integration numbers above; the notification cap only removes writes.
- **Cloud Run CPU and memory, cold-start time** and the instance count under the stranger load (one local process): emulators only, the Cloud Run ms column is an estimate. Three instances would triple the refresh lines (each instance refreshes alone), which is the 4,320 ceiling.
- **Every call rate and the 5% protected share** are inputs; **the real per-tab means of the own-copy allowance** have no traffic behind them. The `privacy.uids` fieldOverride is not enforced by the emulator: the dev rollout (W3-38) must show it READY, and the first real `protected_set_reads` and Part B `fs_reads` come from there.
- A user following 600 accounts, a post receiving 50 likes/s and a cold-start wave are not Part B shapes (Part B adds no counter and no fan-out on the read path); the shapes that are, a viral protected account (500 requests in 0.6 s) and a stranger herd on the set, are above.

Verdict (W3-37): every Part B RPC held its documented budget or the founder-accepted bound (Respond standalone maximum 4 against 5, own-copy allowance priced at its upper bound); Part B alone is about INR 1.4 / 4.4 / 12.7 a month at 300 / 1,000 / 3,000 DAU against the ADR's 1.4 / 4.2 / 10, and it moves the free-read crossover by 2 DAU (177 to 175). The whole product is already past the free read quota under the approved pay-per-use plan (ADR-0022), so Part B is judged on its margin and its RPC budgets, not on the free line.

BUDGET: HOLDS

## 14. W7-23 measurements (2026-10-29, emulators only; nothing was run against dzeroth-dev, dzeroth-prod or any cloud project)
Machine: Windows 11 Home, Intel Core i5-1240P (16 logical processors), 16 GB RAM, Go 1.27.0 toolchain building the API from `main` 7a41ea6b (W7-4 to W7-12 merged, including the #445 takedown reach, the #448 cache isolation and the #451 daily caps; **W7-27 and W7-33 are not merged**, so the first page is 24 / 54 and a send reads the cached recipient graph), k6 2.2.0, firebase-tools with Firestore, Auth, Pub/Sub and Storage on their own port block (Firestore 46080, Auth 46099, Pub/Sub 46085, Storage 46199, hub 46400, logging 46500, API 46081) through a temporary `--config` that was deleted before the commit and behind `scripts/with-private-tmp.sh`; no other session's emulator was touched. **One local API process is one "instance"**, started fresh for the first run; the emulator and the API share the host with k6, so there is no network and no Firestore round trip in any timing below. Emulator timings are indicative only.

Scenario `loadtest/messaging.js` (new; the header documents every phase and knob), run as `make loadtest SCENARIO=messaging` with `API_URL` and `AUTH_EMULATOR_HOST` in the environment, **twice back to back** (7.7 minutes of load each, after a setup that mints 329 users, 348 follows and 292 seed messages), `node loadtest/analyze_messaging.js` for the reads and server times, `node loadtest/messaging_sizes.js` for the bytes. API environment: `ENV=local FEATURE_MESSAGING=on FEATURE_GRAPH=on` (notifications off, so no push), the defaults `CACHE_TTL=60s` and `MESSAGES_PUSHES_PER_RECIPIENT_PER_HOUR=20`, and these raised for the run only: `RATE_LIMIT_PER_IP_PER_MIN=1000000 RATE_LIMIT_PRE_AUTH_IP_PER_MIN=1000000 RATE_LIMIT_PER_USER_PER_MIN=100000 RATE_LIMIT_MESSAGES_SEND_PER_MIN=100000 RATE_LIMIT_MESSAGES_READ_PER_MIN=100000 MESSAGES_SENDS_PER_DAY=1000000 MESSAGES_NEW_ACCOUNT_SENDS_PER_DAY=1000000 MESSAGES_MARK_READ_PER_DAY=1000000 MESSAGES_MUTATIONS_PER_DAY=1000000 RATE_LIMIT_GRAPH_FOLLOW_PER_MIN=100000 QUOTA_FOLLOWS_PER_DAY=100000 QUOTA_NEW_ACCOUNT_FOLLOWS_PER_DAY=100000 QUOTA_DM_NEW_CONVERSATIONS_PER_DAY=100000 QUOTA_NEW_ACCOUNT_DM_NEW_CONVERSATIONS_PER_DAY=100000 READ_BUDGET_PER_UID_PER_DAY=10000000`. Every pair of users follows each other, so every conversation is ACCEPTED (the 3-message request cap never applies).

**Load on the machine (a 5 s sampler of the whole machine, outside k6 and the API):** the machine had been heavily loaded earlier in the day by other sessions; during these runs it was not. Whole-machine CPU busy, mean (max) per phase, run 1 / run 2: setup 5.8% (10) / 6.5% (15.6); cold pages 6.2% (11.5) / 7.0% (14.4); solo empty poll 4.7% (12.2) / 6.5% (14.1); 300-user poll herd 9.1% (39.8) / 8.5% (19.1); send burst 5.8% (7.9) / 6.4% (9.5). Free RAM was tight: 3.9 GB free at rest and **1.9 GB / 1.7 GB at the lowest** in the herd, of 16 GB. So the timings are neither a quiet-machine nor a loaded-machine number; the two runs agree, which is the only claim made.

### Phases, in order (the markers `W723 PHASE` cut the API log)
`cold_pages` at 70 s after setup (every per-instance cache older than the 60 s TTL), `empty_poll` (one user polls a quiet thread every 200 ms for 60 s, nothing else running), `poll_herd` (300 virtual users, one per poller, each polling its own conversation every 15 s for 240 s, after a jittered first poll, plus 1 trickle send a second over the 150 conversations), `send_burst` (50 sends a second for 20 s into one conversation, alternating the sender, plus one poller at 1 a second), `verify` (pages the whole burst thread and compares it with both unread counts).

### Reads, writes and deletes per call (the `fs_reads` / `fs_writes` of the request line; run 1 and run 2 are identical unless two numbers are given)
| Case | calls | reads | writes | Plan (worst / planning) | Within +/- 25%? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ListConversations INBOX, first page of 20 (hub, 20 conversations), cold | 1 | **23** | 0 | 23 / 9 (at 6 rows) | yes (the worst-case row, exact) |
| the same page again (warm) | 1 | **21** (20 rows + the requests `count()`) | 0 | 23 | yes |
| ListConversations `since`, nothing new, warm | 1 | **1** | 0 | 3 / 2 (W7-20 cold: 2) | yes (lower) |
| ListConversations REQUESTS, empty folder | 1 | **1** | 0 | 22 (the page less the `count()`) | yes |
| ListMessages first page of 20, cold (conversation not cached) / warm | 1 / 1 | **24** / **20** | 0 | 24 / 15 | yes |
| ListMessages older page of 20 | 1 | **20** | 0 | 24 | yes |
| ListMessages first page of 50, cold / warm | 1 / 1 | **54** / **50** | 0 | 54 | yes |
| ListMessages first page of a 1-message thread, cold | 1 / 1 | **5** | 0 | 4 + rows | yes |
| **ListMessages `since` poll, quiet thread, solo (60 s, every 200 ms)** | 292 / 290 | **1 in every poll**, `results` 1 (the newest message again) | 0 | 3 cold / 1.2 | yes |
| **ListMessages `since` poll, 300-user herd (15 s interval, 240 s)** | **4,500 / 4,496** | **mean 1.41** / 1.41: 1 read 3,028 / 3,041, 2 reads 1,107 / 1,088, 3 reads 356 / 360, 4 reads 9 / 7; the first poll of each user 1.11, every later one 1.43; `results` mean 1.13 / 1.12 (1: 88%, 2: 11%, 3: 0.7%) | 0 | 3 cold / **1.2** | **yes: +17%** |
| ListMessages thread open in the herd (first page; about half of the opens found the pair's conversation and profiles cached by the partner's open) | 300 | mean 3.47 / 3.45 (2 reads 153, 5 reads 147) | 0 | 24 / 15 | yes (a 1-message thread) |
| ListMessages poll right after a first page of a 60-message thread seeded in about 1 s | 1 | 20 (a full page: the whole thread is inside the 5 s settle window of its newest message) | 0 | 3 + 1 per message | yes (the "+1 per message returned" rule) |
| MarkConversationRead (own entry) | 151 / 151 | **0** (interceptor cached) in all but the cold case (1, W7-20) | **1** in every call | 1 / 0.3 | yes |
| SendMessage, accepted conversation, burst (1,001 sends) | 1,001 / 1,001 | **1** in 1,000, 5 in one (a cold instance) | **3** | 5 / 2.5 | yes |
| SendMessage, accepted conversation, trickle between 150 pairs | 241 / 240 | **mean 2.19 / 2.20**: 1 read 102, 3 reads 130, 2 to 6 reads 9 | **3** | 5 / 2.5 | yes (-12%) |
| SendMessage, new conversation (setup, 174 of them) | 174 | **7** in 155, 5 in 19 | **5** | 7 | yes (exact) |
| Eraser step, reference account (20 conversations, 300 own messages) | W7-20 | **381** | **360**, 20 deletes | 340 / 340 / 20 | **corrected** (+12%, +6%) |
| Export section `messages`, same account | W7-20 | **620** | 0 | 620 | yes (exact) |
| Takedown, non-participant probe, GetMe badge, Accept, Delete, Report | W7-20 | 4 reads and at most 4 writes; 1 read (2 with the interceptor); 3 cold / 0 warm; 3 and 3; 6 cold / 3 warm | | as in §2 | yes |

- **Every number above is at or under its documented ceiling.** The only call above its planning value is the poll (1.41 against 1.2), and it is the only line of the model that moved (+1.05 reads per DAU of 38.05). **The poll is a single-instance number**: the conversation copy and the caller's profile and graph are cached per instance for 60 s, and a 15 s poll keeps them warm, so 88% of polls were one read. With three warm instances (`max-instances` 3) each user's entries are cached on each instance they reach, so more polls will land cold at 3 reads; the all-cold ceiling is 15 reads per DAU for 5 polls against the 7.05 modelled, +8 reads per DAU (+2.5% of the whole product). The first real number is `poll_reads` of §9 on the W7-24 dev allowlist.
- **A poll of a quiet thread is never empty and never cheaper than 1 read.** The `since` token trails the newest message by the 5 s settle window, so every poll returns the newest message again (`results` 1: 292 of 292 in the solo phase), 1 read warm, about 250 bytes of binary proto. Reads are the same as an empty query (the minimum is 1), so this costs bytes, not reads. **In a conversation that is being written to the poll reads every message of the last 5 s**: the burst poller (1 a second, page 50) read 50 on 24 of 25 polls and got a `gap_page_token` on all 24 (a client then pages the gap: more requests), so 1,202 messages were read for 1,001 sent. At human rates this is small (one message every 5 s makes each 15 s poll read about 4), and it is the "settle-window re-delivery" the W7-20 report asked about; it is inside the "+1 read per message returned" rule and in the 1.41.
- **Cold and warm first pages** are exact: reads = rows + 3 (inbox) or rows + 4 (thread) cold, rows + 1 (inbox) or rows (thread) warm. The plan's 15 for a thread open is therefore a statement about the mean page (about 11 messages), not about the page size.

### Server time (`latency_ms` of the request line, an integer number of ms; k6 `http_req_duration` agrees: Poll p95 6.4 ms and 9.4 ms)
| Case | n (run 1 / run 2) | p50 | p95 | p99 | max |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Empty poll, solo** (a quiet thread, one user, 5 a second, nothing else running) | 292 / 290 | **4 / 5 ms** | 5 / 8 | 7 / 9 | 7 / 10 |
| Poll in the 300-user herd (18.8 polls a second) | 4,500 / 4,496 | 3 / 4 | 5 / 8 | 6 / 10 | 10 / 16 |
| SendMessage in the 50/s burst | 1,001 / 1,001 | 3 / 3 | 5 / 4 | 6 / 6 | 13 / 21 |
| SendMessage, trickle | 241 / 240 | 4 / 5 | 7 / 8 | 8 / 12 | 20 / 43 |
| ListConversations first page, cold (20 rows) | 1 / 1 | 12 / 13 | | | |
| ListMessages first page, cold (a 1-message thread) | 1 / 1 | 10 / 10 | | | |

- **What the empty-poll time replaces, and what it does not.** The 0.1 s per request of §1 was an upper bound for every request. The measured server time of a poll is 4 to 5 ms (p95 5 to 8) with no network and no Firestore round trip. On Cloud Run a poll of a warm conversation is one Firestore query in Mumbai from Mumbai: I would expect **about 10 to 20 ms (one round trip), 30 to 50 ms cold (three sequential reads)**, derived and not measured. Cloud Run bills request-based instance time in 100 ms steps (recalled from the pricing page, to be re-verified; §7 has no rounding line) and a request that shares its step with another bills nothing extra. **So the poll's vCPU is a range, not a swap: INR 4 (the measured 4 ms, fully overlapped) to INR 21 (20 ms) to INR 104 (100 ms billed alone) a month at 3,000 DAU for 5 polls per DAU.** At 3,000 DAU the product averages 1.8 requests a second, so most requests bill their own 100 ms; the overlap that gets to the lower figure needs about 10 requests a second in the same instance, which the 300-user herd (20 polls a second) has and the daily average does not. §5 keeps 0.1 s and shows the lower rows.

### Send burst: 50 sends a second into one conversation (the rule-3 counter-contention trigger)
| | run 1 | run 2 |
|---|---|---|
| Sends, 20 s at 50 a second, alternating X and Y | 1,001 | 1,001 |
| HTTP 200 / any other status | **1,001 / 0** | **1,001 / 0** |
| `txn_attempts` | **1 in all 1,001** | **1 in all 1,001** |
| WARN `messaging_*_contention` lines, `UNAVAILABLE busy` answers, ERROR lines | 0, 0, 0 | 0, 0, 0 |
| Reads / writes per send | 1 (one send 5) / 3 | 1 (one send 5) / 3 |
| Thread after the burst, paged 21 times at 50 | 1,002 messages (the seed + 1,001), 1,002 unique ids | 1,002, 1,002 unique |
| Messages by X / by Y | 502 / 500 | 502 / 500 |
| Unread of X / of Y | **500 / 502** | **500 / 502** |
| Consistent (X unread = messages by Y, Y unread = messages by X, no duplicate id) | **yes** | **yes** |
- **No lost message and no lost `unreadCount` Increment at 50 sends a second into one pair, and no retry of any transaction.** The send transaction reads the conversation document and creates the message and updates the two inbox entries with `Increment`; the conversation document is read but not written on a send (accepted state), so two sends do not contend on it.
- **That proves nothing about production.** The emulator does not enforce Firestore's per-document write limit (about 1 sustained write a second) and its locking is not Firestore's, so 0 aborts here says the code path is race-free, not that a hot inbox entry would hold. **The real ceiling is the config, not the load:** `RATE_LIMIT_MESSAGES_SEND_PER_MIN` 20 per user per instance x 2 users x 3 instances is 120 a minute, 2 sends a second into one conversation, and each send writes both entries, so one inbox entry can take **up to 2 `Increment` writes a second sustained** (and 1 a second from the other participant alone), above rule 3's 1 a second; the daily cap of 300 sends x 3 instances per account bounds it to a few minutes a day. A flagged finding for the architect (shard `unreadCount` for an entry that takes more than 1 a second, or a per-conversation cap), not something this ticket changes.

### Response sizes behind the 10 KB per DAU per day (`node loadtest/messaging_sizes.js`, body bytes counted on the socket, headers excluded; the API gzips whenever the client sends `Accept-Encoding: gzip`)
| Call | JSON | JSON gzip | binary proto | **proto gzip (the app)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SendMessage (accepted conversation) | 752 | 412 | 347 | **255** |
| ListConversations first page, 20 conversations | 9,524 | 2,215 | 3,890 | **2,169** |
| ListConversations first page, 6 conversations (the model mean) | 3,041 | 1,038 | 1,323 | **902** |
| ListConversations `since`, nothing new | 101 | 126 | 86 | **111** |
| ListMessages first page, 12 messages (the model mean) | 3,446 | 1,224 | 2,054 | **1,126** |
| ListMessages first page, 20 / 50 messages | 5,278 / 12,219 | 1,636 / 2,503 | 3,114 / 7,164 | **1,587 / 2,526** |
| **ListMessages poll, quiet thread (the newest message again)** | 367 | 329 | 250 | **275** |
| ListMessages poll, 12 messages in the settle window | 5,071 | 1,529 | 3,075 | **1,583** |
| MarkConversationRead | 2 | 27 | 0 | **20** |
- **Per DAU per day, from the call mix of §1 and these bodies: 3.5 KB in binary proto after gzip (4.2 KB for JSON after gzip)**: sends 0.38, inbox first pages 0.45 and refreshes 0.31, thread opens 0.56 and resumes 0.31, polls 1.44 (5 x 275 B plus 13% of them with a second message), mark-read 0.02. Pushes are FCM and the Pub/Sub push response is internal, so neither is internet egress. Add response headers (about 190 bytes measured with `curl -i` locally, about 250 assumed on Cloud Run) on the 11.1 client responses a day: 2.8 KB, **about 6.3 KB (proto) to 7.0 KB (JSON) a day in all, against the plan's 10 KB**; TLS and TCP framing are not counted. The model prices the 10 KB (an upper bound by about 30%, INR 3 a month at 3,000 DAU) until the first Cloud Run `sent_bytes` actuals are in §8. The server gzips even 2-byte bodies (the Mark and `since` rows are larger gzipped than not); a minimum size for compression would save under 0.05 KB a day, not worth a ticket.

### Storage (derived, not measured: the emulator has no storage meter)
A message document is about 351 bytes (name 105 + fields 214 + 32) plus six automatic single-field index entries (`senderId`, `state`, `createdAt`, ascending and descending; `text` and `requestHash` are exempt) of about 152 bytes: **about 1.26 KB**. At 1.5 messages a day and 0.075 new conversations a day (about 4 KB for the conversation and the two inbox entries), **2.2 KB per DAU per day**; the 0.5 GiB trigger of §6 is 815 days at 300 DAU, 245 at 1,000, 82 at 3,000. Measure it, once messaging has real data, with the collection-group `count()` of `messages` x 1.26 KB.

### Owed at W7-24: send-to-render p95 on dev (needs messaging on in dev, which is W7-24; nothing was done to `dzeroth-dev` here)
**Method.** Two allowlisted dev accounts, A (sender, any client) and B (the web client, signed in, the thread open and visible so the poller runs at its hot 15 s). About 20 sends from A at random offsets (uniform 0 to 30 s apart, so the sends are not phase-locked to B's poll). For each send: `t_server` is the `createdAt` of the `SendMessage` response (the server commit time); `t_render` is the client time at which B's UI first draws the message (a debug log line or a `performance.mark` in `ThreadPoller` when the page is applied); latency = `t_render` minus `t_server`. Bound the clock skew with the `Date` header of B's poll response against B's clock (the Date header has 1 s resolution) and report p50, p95 and max. **Expected** about 0 to 15 s uniformly (p95 about 14.3 s) plus the round trip (0.1 to 0.5 s), so **p95 near 15 s against the 20 s trigger, about 5 s of headroom**; a p95 above 18 s means the poller is late (a throttled hidden tab, retries) and is the finding. Android with notifications on should render a push-triggered pull within a few seconds (a separate number, not the trigger). Cost to dev: about 20 sends x 10 reads plus 20 minutes of polls at 15 s (80 x 1.4) is under 400 Firestore reads. Also at W7-24: the first real `poll_reads` and `msg_reads` of §9, and the `changedAt DESC` inbox query against the real index (the emulator does not enforce indexes).

**Measured at W7-24 (dev, 2026-10-30; API smoke with the dev test accounts, not a browser run).** n = 20 sends by w7smoke2 at random 0 to 30 s gaps (13:29:42 to 13:35:15 UTC), read by an API poller as w7smoke1 that emulates the web hot poll (15 s). Matched 20 of 20. Server-side send to delivery: **p50 7.9 s, p95 15.5 s, max 16.9 s** (nearest rank). The omitted client render and return trip are 0.1 to 0.5 s, so a client-side p95 is near 15.6 to 16.0 s: about 4.5 s under the 20 s trigger and under the 18 s late-poller line. Android push-to-screen is not measured (leg 5 owed). Poll reads, first real figure: 23 polls over the window, mean `fs_reads` 2.09 and mean `results` 2.09 on one busy thread, against the 1.41 modelled (an idle poll costs 1 read; the re-returned newest message costs one, because the since token trails by SettleWindow). One thread is not a fleet mean, so the model is not replaced here; sre-performance to re-check the poll line at the first DAU-scale window. `msg_reads` per DAU is not computed (two testers is not a DAU measure). The measured SendMessage maximum of 7 reads (new conversation) is above the 5 worst cold in `docs/runbooks/messaging.md` section 4; it is not explained yet and is owed to backend-developer and sre-performance before the W7 budget is re-stated. The Chrome render was not captured, so the in-app render time is not in these numbers.

### What was not measured
- **Push delivery under load** (the local API has no push wiring and `notifications` was off): the 4 / 7 / 2 / 0 reads of W7-20 stand, 3.5 planning, a request per message.
- **Cloud Run CPU and memory, cold start, the instance count** under the herd, and **real Firestore latency and contention**: one local process on emulators. Three instances would cache each user's entries three times (more polls at 3 reads, above) and would multiply every per-instance limit by three.
- **Send-to-render p95** (above) and **the real frequency of polls inside a settle window** at human message rates: both need dev with two accounts.
- **Every call rate** (1.5 sends, 5 polls and the rest) is an input with no traffic behind it; the herd was 300 users polling a quiet thread, which fixes the per-poll cost, not the share of polls in the product.
- **A user following 600 accounts, a viral post at 50 likes a second and a cold-start wave** are not messaging shapes (messaging reads no timeline and no counter on a post); the messaging shapes that are, a 50 sends/s burst into one pair and a 300-user poll herd, are above. A **notification fan-out burst** is the push path, not run for the reason above.
- The **storage** line and the Cloud Run **100 ms rounding** are derived or recalled, flagged where used.

Verdict (W7-23): every messaging RPC held its documented read, write and delete budget in both runs (the only line above its planning value is the poll, 1.41 against 1.2, inside the +/- 25% and the 3-read ceiling); the stale eraser figure is corrected (381 / 360 / 20); the burst shows no retry, no lost message and no lost unread increment; and W7 alone is about INR 12.5 / 59 / 484 a month at 300 / 1,000 / 3,000 DAU against the plan's 12 / 58 / 480 (INR 384 to 484 at 3,000 DAU across the poll billing range). At the 300-DAU target the writes are 74% of the 16k line (under 80%) and the reads, already over before W7, are the approved pay-per-use line (ADR-0022). Two follow-ups are not defects of this ticket and are for the architect: the 2-writes-a-second ceiling on one inbox entry at the D13 caps (rule 3), and W7-27 and W7-33, which add +2.0 reads per DAU when they land (INR 6 a month at 3,000 DAU). The send-to-render p95 is owed at W7-24.

BUDGET: HOLDS

## 15. W4-23 measurements (2026-10-31, emulators only; nothing was run against dzeroth-dev, dzeroth-prod or any cloud project)
Machine: Windows 11 Home, Intel Core i5-1240P (16 logical processors), 16 GB RAM, Go 1.27.0 building the API from `main` be90d1bd (W4-5, W4-7/8 and W4-9/9b/10 merged; W4-11/12 (F2 names) are conditional and not built), k6 2.2.0, firebase-tools with Firestore, Auth, Pub/Sub and Storage on their own port block (Firestore 59080, Auth 59099, Storage 59199, hub 59400, logging 59500, Pub/Sub a fresh port per run in 59086 to 59091, API 59081) through a temporary `--config` kept outside the repository and behind `scripts/with-private-tmp.sh`; no other session's emulator was touched. **One local API process is one "instance"** (a fresh process per run). The emulator and the API share the host with k6, so there is no network and no Firestore round trip in any timing below, and other sessions were using the machine: timings are indicative only, reads are exact.

Scenario `loadtest/search.js` (new; the header documents every phase and knob), run as `make loadtest SCENARIO=search` with `API_URL` and `AUTH_EMULATOR_HOST` in the environment, **three times** (run 1 had `FEATURE_ENGAGEMENT` and `FEATURE_BOOKMARKS` off and a MEDIA tag with no `media` array, which is why it is not used: the budget counts the `userLikes` read, and MEDIA keeps only posts with `len(media) > 0`, not `hasMedia`; runs 2 and 3 are the recorded ones and agree to the read except the one case run 3 added, below). Each run: 60 users, 2,100 posts (6 tags of 40 posts, tag 3 of 70, a hot tag of 40, the rest filler over 40 trending tags; every third tag post carries one image written through the emulator REST API), about 8 minutes of load after a 70 s idle (> `CACHE_TTL` 60 s). `node loadtest/analyze_search.js` for reads and server times, `node loadtest/search_sizes.js` for the bytes. API environment: `ENV=local FEATURE_SEARCH=on FEATURE_TRENDS=on FEATURE_POSTS=on FEATURE_GRAPH=on FEATURE_ENGAGEMENT=on FEATURE_BOOKMARKS=on TRENDS_MIN_INTERVAL=30s` (the other defaults: `CACHE_TTL=60s`, `CACHE_SEARCH_PREFIX_ENTRIES=2000`, `CACHE_SEARCH_TAG_ENTRIES=500`, `TRENDS_MAX_POSTS_PER_RUN=2000`), and these raised for the run only: `RATE_LIMIT_PER_IP_PER_MIN=1000000 RATE_LIMIT_PRE_AUTH_IP_PER_MIN=1000000 RATE_LIMIT_PER_USER_PER_MIN=100000 RATE_LIMIT_SEARCH_PEOPLE_PER_MIN=100000 RATE_LIMIT_SEARCH_POSTS_PER_MIN=100000 SEARCH_CALLS_PER_DAY=1000000 READ_BUDGET_PER_UID_PER_DAY=10000000 RATE_LIMIT_POST_CREATE_PER_MIN=100000 QUOTA_POSTS_PER_DAY=1000000 QUOTA_NEW_ACCOUNT_POSTS_PER_DAY=1000000`. k6: 0 unexpected responses; the only failed requests are the 3 deliberate VALIDATION rejections (400). No ERROR line in any run; the one WARN is `trends_backlog` (the 2,000-post cap, below).

### Phases, in order (the markers `W4 PHASE` and `W4 STEP` cut the API log)
`cold_calls` (70 s after setup, one user, 27 serial steps, every cache older than the TTL), `refresh` (60 s later: seven calls to the two `/internal/cron` routes, 62 s of waits), `trends` and `trends_herd` (4 calls, then 30 a second for 40 s from 60 viewers), `typeahead_burst` (600 `SearchPeople` limit-5 calls in 15 s), `hot_tag` (50 new posts a minute on one tag and 8 `SearchPosts` a second for 75 s).

### Reads per call against the plan (the `fs_reads` of the request line; interceptor included; runs 2 and 3 are identical unless two numbers are given)
| Case | calls | reads (writes 0 in every row) | Plan (worst / warm) | Within +/- 25%? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SearchPeople limit 5, everything cold (5 rows) | 1 | **7** | 7 | yes (exact) |
| the same call again, same viewer | 1 | **0** (`cache_hit` true) | 0 to 2 | yes |
| the same prefix, another viewer (entry warm, viewer cold) | 1 | **2** (interceptor + graph) | 0 to 2 | yes |
| SearchPeople limit 20, cold (20 rows) / same viewer again / another viewer | 1 / 1 / 1 | **22** / **0** / **2** | 22 / 0 to 2 | yes (exact) |
| SearchPeople, a full handle (1 row), cold / another viewer | 1 / 1 | **3** / **2** | 1 + 1 + rows | yes |
| SearchPeople, a prefix nobody has (0 rows), cold | 1 | **2** (interceptor + the minimum 1; the graph read is skipped) | "an empty entry skips the graph read" | yes |
| SearchPeople `a` (VALIDATION `query`), cold viewer / warm viewer | 1 / 1 | **1** / **0** | "0 reads" | yes: the 1 is the account-status interceptor, which every call pays; the search itself reads nothing |
| SearchPeople `hello world` (unsupported), cold viewer / warm viewer | 1 / 1 | **1** / **0** (`query_kind` unsupported) | "0 Firestore reads" | yes (same reading) |
| SearchPosts TOP page 1, cold (20 rows of the 30-post entry) | 1 | **33** (run 1, `userLikes` off: 32) | 33 | yes (exact) |
| LATEST and MEDIA page 1 right after, same viewer (one entry serves all three) | 1 / 1 | **0** / **0** (MEDIA: 10 rows) | 0 to 3 | yes |
| TOP page 1, another viewer (entry warm) | 1 | **3** (interceptor, graph, `userLikes`) | 0 to 3 | yes |
| TOP page 1 again, same viewer | 1 | **0** | 0 | yes |
| LATEST page 1 at page_size 50, cold / again (40 posts exist) | 1 / 1 | **43** / **40** (3 + rows, so **53** at 50 rows) | 53 | yes (the cap is by formula; the tag held 40 posts) |
| LATEST older page of 20, viewer already warm | 1 | **20** (a cold viewer pays the 3 of the TOP row: **23**, derived) | 23 | yes |
| MEDIA page 1, cold (the 10 image posts of the entry) | 1 | **33** | 33 | yes (exact) |
| MEDIA older scan at the 30-post cap, viewer warm (run 3, tag of 70 posts) | 1 | **30** (10 rows; **33** for a cold viewer, derived); with 10 older posts left (run 2): 10 | 33 | yes |
| SearchPosts `@someone` (unsupported), cold viewer / `#123`, warm / 60-character tag (VALIDATION), warm | 1 / 1 / 1 | **1** / **0** / **0** | interceptor only | yes |
| **GetTrends, cold instance and cold viewer** | 1 | **2** (interceptor + `trends/current`), `cache_hit` false | 2 | yes (exact) |
| GetTrends, same viewer / another viewer / with `prefix` | 1 / 1 / 1 | **0** / **1** (the interceptor; the document is cached) / **0** | 0 to 1 | yes |
| **GetTrends herd: 1,200 and 1,201 calls in 40 s from 60 viewers** | 1,200 / 1,201 | **38 reads in all (mean 0.03)**, every one a viewer's first call; **0 document reads** | one document read per instance per 10 min | yes: the document was read once, by the cold call before the herd |
| **Trend refresh, 2,100 posts waiting** (`trends_refresh`) | 1 | **2,001 reads, 1 write**, 2,000 posts scanned, 39 tags counted, 16 kept, `backlog` true, WARN `trends_backlog`; 128 / 238 ms | 1 + new posts (<= 2,001), 1 write | yes (exact: 1 read per post + the document) |
| the run after that (the 100 left) | 1 | **101** reads, 1 write, `backlog` false, 18 ms | 1 + new posts | yes |
| a run inside `TRENDS_MIN_INTERVAL` (three calls: 2 trends-refresh, 1 daily-maintenance) | 3 | **1** read each, 0 writes, `reason=fresh` | 1 | yes |
| a run with nothing new (daily-maintenance, 31 s later) | 1 | **2** reads (the document + the empty page's minimum 1), 1 write, **the list is replaced by an empty one** | 1 + new posts | yes; see the finding below |
| a run with 120 new posts over 12 tags | 1 | **121** reads, 1 write, 12 tags kept, 17 to 26 ms | 1 + new posts | yes |

- **Every number is at or under its documented ceiling, and the plan's per-RPC table needs no correction.** The cold figures are exact formulas (`1 + 1 + limit` for people, `1 + 1 + 1 + 30` for a tag entry, `3 + rows` for an uncached page); the warm figures are the viewer's own components (interceptor, graph, `userLikes`), 0 to 3.
- **The plan's "typical" column does move, and it is a statement about hit rates, not about calls.** Typeahead 4.0 holds as a session of three keystroke calls: the first call is viewer-cold (7), the next two pay rows only on a cold prefix (about 3 and 2), (7 + 3 + 2) / 3 = 4.0. The People tab 12 is exactly a 50% prefix-hit rate (0.5 x 22 + 0.5 x 2); at no sharing it is 22. SearchPosts page 1 at the plan's 40% tag-entry hit rate is 0.4 x 3 + 0.6 x 33 = **21.0, not 19** (+10.5%, inside the 25% line). Older pages and Media scans are 20 / 30 for a warm viewer (the usual case, one page after another) and 23 / 33 for a cold one; with a quarter of them Media scans (an **assumption**) the typical is **24.0 against 23** (+4%). **The hit rates themselves are assumptions with no traffic behind them** and are the one input that moves the line (below).
- **Corrected W4 total: 38.9 reads per DAU per day (plan 36.6, +6.3%)**: 6.0 + 3.6 + 21.0 + 7.2 (0.3 x 24.0) + 0.1 + 1.0, 0 writes, 0 deletes, **6.6 requests** (the plan's 5.1 omitted the GetTrends call the typeahead makes on each debounced `#tag` or word, below). The plan's table is corrected in the same PR.
- **The sensitivity to the tag-entry hit rate h is the model's real uncertainty.** Page 1 costs 33 - 30h reads, so the W4 total is **50.9 at h = 0, 38.9 at 40%, 29.9 at 70%**. At 300 DAU the chance that two calls for the same tag land on the same instance inside 60 s is small unless the demand is concentrated, and it is concentrated by design only where trends send everyone to the same ten tags (Explore, the side column). So 40% is a reasonable guess for the trend-driven share and optimistic for typed searches; the `cache_hit` field of the request line is the real measurement (trigger 4, §6).

### Hot tag, typeahead burst and herd (server behaviour under the shapes the plan names)
| Shape | calls | reads | What it shows |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hot tag**: 50 new posts a minute on one tag and 8 SearchPosts a second (TOP and LATEST page 1) from 40 viewers, 75 s | 601 | **239 and 240 in all (mean 0.40)**: **2 entry loads of 30 posts** (TOP 32 and TOP 32 in run 2, TOP 32 and LATEST 31 in run 3; a third caller that arrived during a load paid 1), about 598 hits at 0.26 (LATEST) and 0.32 to 0.33 (TOP) a call, which is the viewers' own graph and `userLikes` re-reads at the 60 s TTL | The entry reloads once per 60 s per instance however many users search it, so the cost of a hot tag is **30 reads a minute per instance, 43,200 a day, 129,600 at 3 instances (INR 129 a month for one tag searched round the clock on every instance)**; the 50 posts a minute do not add reads (they appear within the 60 s bound) |
| **Typeahead burst**: 600 SearchPeople limit-5 calls in 15 s (40 a second) from 60 viewers, half progressive prefixes of one shared handle and half random 2-character prefixes | 600 / 601 | **364 / 356 (mean 0.61 / 0.59)**: 321 / 329 hits at 0.11, 279 / 272 misses at 1.18 (mostly empty prefixes, 1 read, and six simultaneous first calls on the shared prefix at 6 each) | A prefix that nobody shares costs 1 read when it matches nothing and `rows` when it does; 6 concurrent first callers each paid the 5-row query (the people entry has no singleflight, the tag entry does): at most `viewers x (1 + limit)` extra reads for one cold prefix, bounded by the 90-per-minute people bucket |
| **GetTrends herd**: 30 a second for 40 s | 1,200 / 1,201 | **38** (viewers' first calls) | the document is read once per instance per 10 min |

### Server time (`latency_ms` of the request line; k6 `http_req_duration` agrees: p95 11.3 ms over the whole of run 3)
| Case | n | p50 | p95 | max |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SearchPeople, prefix entry hit (typeahead burst) | 321 / 329 | 1 | 2 | 8 / 3 |
| SearchPeople, prefix entry miss (typeahead burst) | 279 / 272 | 4 / 3 | 6 / 5 | 16 / 24 |
| SearchPosts page 1, tag entry hit (hot tag) | 598 | 1 | 5 / 2 | 15 / 6 |
| SearchPosts page 1, tag entry miss (cold TOP, 30-post load) | 1 each | 88 | | |
| GetTrends, warm (herd) | 1,200 / 1,201 | 1 | 2 | 5 / 18 |
| Trend refresh, 2,000 posts, 1 write | 1 | 128 / 238 ms | | |
- **Cold-load times are the only ones that matter for the 400 ms target and the emulator cannot speak for them**: a tag entry load is one indexed 30-document query (88 ms here with no network); on Cloud Run in Mumbai from Mumbai I would expect 40 to 80 ms (derived, not measured). **The refresh at the cap took 128 to 699 ms against the step's 10 s cap and the route's 25 s deadline**; real Firestore is about 10 to 20 ms per 500-document page, so about 4 pages of 500 is under 1 s there as well (derived).

### Response sizes behind the egress line (`node loadtest/search_sizes.js`, body bytes counted on the socket, headers excluded; the API gzips whenever the client sends `Accept-Encoding: gzip`; 34 hashtag posts of about 90 characters from 10 authors with pseudo-word text, one post in three with one image)
| Call | JSON | JSON gzip | binary proto | **proto gzip (the app)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SearchPeople limit 5 (5 rows) | 987 | 350 | 481 | **302** |
| SearchPeople limit 20 (20 rows) | 3,913 | 973 | 1,922 | **938** |
| SearchPeople, empty / unsupported | 2 / 25 | 27 / 50 | 0 / 2 | **20 / 27** |
| SearchPosts TOP page 1 (20 rows; `page_size` 30 also returns 20, `topMaxPage`) | 11,482 | 2,363 | 6,469 | **2,376** |
| SearchPosts LATEST page 1, 20 / 34 rows | 11,609 / 19,483 | 2,473 / 3,420 | 6,579 / 10,976 | **2,480 / 3,559** |
| SearchPosts MEDIA page 1 (10 rows) | 7,918 | 1,800 | 4,919 | **1,739** |
| SearchPosts, unsupported | 25 | 50 | 2 | **27** |
| GetTrends limit 10 / limit 30 (12 trends exist) | 527 / 671 | 171 / 185 | 203 / 260 | **123 / 143** |
- **Per DAU per day, from the call mix: 1.5 x 302 + 0.3 x 938 + 1.0 x 2,400 + 0.3 x 2,480 + 2.0 x 123 = 4.1 KB in binary proto after gzip; plus the typeahead's GetTrends call with a `prefix` (1.5 x 143 bytes) and about 250 bytes of response headers on each of the 6.6 responses (1.65 KB): about 6.0 KB a day against the plan's 41 KB.** In JSON without gzip it is about 20.7 KB (987 x 1.5 + 3,913 x 0.3 + 11,482 + 11,609 x 0.3 + 527 x 3.5 + 1.65 KB of headers), so the plan's 41 KB is not reachable by any codec in this build; it was about 6.8 times too high. **The model's 6.0 KB replaces it** (INR 0.6 / 1.9 / 5.8 a month at 300 / 1,000 / 3,000 DAU, was 4 / 13 / 40). The pseudo-word text compresses worse than real language and the repeated image URLs compress better than real ones; the proto-gzip column is within a few percent of the JSON-gzip column in both cases, so the figure is robust to the text.
- **The images are the egress that matters and are not measured here** (the client fetches the `thumb_url` of each result from GCS, outside the API). The plan bounds them at 10 GETs x 15 KB a DAU a day; `cost-model` §1 says thumbnails are about 40 KB and §7 says GCS egress to India is always billed (the plan's "inside the free 100 GB" is the North-America-only allowance). Re-priced at the plan's own 150 KB: INR 14.5 / 48.4 / 145 a month at 300 / 1,000 / 3,000 DAU. With §1's 40 KB thumbs, 1.3 result pages a day, 30% of results with an image and 20 uncached avatars of 4 KB per page (about 0.4 MB a DAU a day, all uncached, so an upper bound that the on-device image cache and Home's overlap lower): **INR 38.8 / 129 / 388**. Both are shown in §5 as unmeasured upper bounds.

### The W4-25..28 corrections (PR #491, read against this run and `main`)
| Claim | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| (1) The client calls GetTrends on every debounced typeahead `#tag` or word, so **6.6 requests per DAU, not 5.1** | **Confirmed.** | `search_suggestions_cubit.dart` (`trendsEnabled && !tagsOff && _longEnough`, 300 ms debounce) calls `getTrends(prefix)` on each debounced query that is a tag or word; the plan's 2.0 GetTrends calls were Explore and the side column only. The 1.5 typeahead sessions add **1.5 requests: 5.1 + 1.5 = 6.6**. Reads do not move: a GetTrends call with a `prefix` measured **0 reads** warm (§15 table) and the document is read once per instance per 10 min; egress rises by 1.5 x (143 + 250) = 0.6 KB (6.0 KB in all). Cloud Run: 58.9 requests per DAU per day in all, so requests run out at **about 1,116 DAU** (from 1,257) and vCPU-s at **about 1,005 DAU** (from 1,131). W4 at 3,000 DAU is **INR 284** on this model's inputs (requests 22.9, vCPU-s 137.3, reads 118.0, egress 5.8); the coordinator's 311 uses the plan's 41 KB egress and the ADR's 275 base, and the difference (+37 for the extra 1.5 requests) is the same. |
| (2) Proposed: GetTrends cold 3 reads (W4-25 hidden list), at most 864 document reads a day at 3 instances; the sweep +500 author reads and +1 per run (W4-26) | **Not measured: not on `main`.** Projected rows in §2, marked "after W4-25/26". | The run measured 2 reads cold and 432 a day at 3 instances (144 refreshes x 3), and a refresh of 2,001 reads. After W4-25 the cold call reads the hidden list too (3, and 864 a day = 2 documents x 144 x 3); after W4-26 a run at the cap reads 2,001 + 500 + 1 = 2,502 (INR 3.0 a month at 1 run a day, still capped). Neither moves the per-DAU line by more than 0.2 reads at 300 DAU (864 / 300 = 2.9 reads per DAU only if every instance stayed warm all day, which scale-to-zero prevents). |
| (3) The read-budget bound is **2,268**, not 2,368; ADR-0028 D9 should say **6,804**, not 7,104 | **Confirmed by the arithmetic.** | `ReadBudgetMaxCallReads` = 269 (`config.go`) and the cap is `READ_BUDGET_PER_UID_PER_DAY` 2,000: a uid spends at most 2,000 - 1 + 269 = **2,268** reads per instance per day, x 3 instances = **6,804**. The comment at `limits.go:145` and ADR-0028 D9 (lines 278 and 367) say 2,368 and 7,104, which add 369 instead of 268. Neither file is changed here (code and the Accepted ADR belong to their owners). No measured per-call figure depends on it: the largest search call is 53 reads. |

### Findings (not defects of this ticket)
1. **Each run's list is computed from its own window, so a run with nothing new writes an empty list.** A `daily-maintenance` call 31 s after a good run (`TRENDS_MIN_INTERVAL` 30 s, so the "fresh" guard had expired) scanned 0 posts and wrote an empty `trends/current` (kept 0), and `GetTrends` answered 0 trends until the next run with posts; likewise run 2 (the 100 posts left after the 2,000-post cap) kept 9 tags against run 1's 16. At the production interval (12 h) a run inside it is skipped (`reason=fresh`, 1 read, measured), and a daily run after a day with no tagged post blanking the list may well be the intended "Trending today" meaning. It matters for F1 A (hourly, 50 minutes): an hour with no new posts would blank the list. For the architect to confirm; nothing here is changed.
2. **The per-run cap binds at about 2,000 DAU, below the 3,000 DAU column.** With the daily run (F1 B) and 1.0 posts per DAU a day, 2,000 posts a day is 2,000 DAU: past it `backlog` is true, the run reads 2,001 and the rest waits for the next run (the WARN `trends_backlog` of the first run above). The cost is capped (2,001 reads a day, INR 2.4 a month) but the list is then built from the oldest 2,000 posts since the watermark, not the newest. This is trigger 3 (§6).
3. **The people prefix entry has no singleflight.** Six simultaneous first calls on one cold prefix each paid the 5-row query (6 reads each). Bounded and small (viewers x limit once per 60 s per prefix per instance); not a ticket.
4. **The Media tab keeps only posts with `len(media) > 0`** (`withOwnMedia`), not `hasMedia`; any load script or seed that sets only the flag measures an empty tab. Noted in the script header.

### What was not measured
- **Cloud Run CPU and memory, cold start, the instance count and real Firestore latency and contention**: one local process on emulators. Three instances would cache each tag and prefix three times, so the tag-entry hit rate (the sensitivity above) is lower at 3 instances than on this one, and every per-instance limit is multiplied by three.
- **The tag-entry and prefix-entry hit rates in production, and every call rate** (1.5 typeahead calls, 1.0 page-1 and the rest) are inputs with no traffic behind them. The dev allowlist (3 uids) at W4-24 gives the first `cache_hit` figures; the real figure needs the MVP ramp.
- **A user following 600 accounts and a viral post at 50 likes a second** are timeline and counter shapes; search reads neither the home timeline nor a post counter (the `userLikes` read is one document per viewer per 60 s). **A notification fan-out burst** is the push path and search emits none. **The shapes that are search's** (a 600-entry typeahead burst, a hot tag with 50 posts a minute, a trends refresh at the 2,000-post cap, a 1,200-call GetTrends herd) are above. **A cold-start wave** is the Cloud Run instance start, not measurable on a local process.
- **The size of a post with images fetched from GCS** (above), the on-device cache that lowers it, and the **real trends-refresh time on Firestore** (derived above).
- **Storage**: `trends/current` is one document of under 100 KB (16 trends of about 40 bytes here); nothing else is written, so the Firestore storage line does not move. Measured nowhere: the emulator has no storage meter.

Verdict (W4-23): every W4 RPC held its documented read, write and delete budget in both recorded runs (all cold figures are exact; the only plan value that moves is the typical SearchPosts page 1, 21.0 against 19, +10.5%, and the older-page typical 24.0 against 23, +4%); the plan's 41 KB egress per DAU is corrected to 6.0 KB and its 5.1 requests to 6.6; W4 alone is about INR 12.4 / 41.3 / 284 a month at 300 / 1,000 / 3,000 DAU against the plan's 15 / 50 / 169 (ADR-0028's restated 275 at 3,000 DAU) with a sensitivity of INR 16 / 53 / 321 at no tag-entry sharing; the reads, already over the free quota at 300 DAU before W4 (243%), are the approved pay-per-use line (ADR-0022), and the line W4 adds is judged on its margin (INR 12.4 at 300 DAU) and its RPC budgets; Cloud Run requests stay at 27% and vCPU-s at 30% of their free quotas at 300 DAU (and vCPU-s reach 99.6% at 1,000 DAU: the first Cloud Run limit is now about 1,005 DAU), and writes do not move (74% of the 16k line). Triggers are named in §6; no alert policy or log-based metric is added. The only unmeasured line that can move the 3,000 DAU bill by more than the whole of W4's own cost is the result-image egress (INR 145 to 388), which is the client's cache behaviour and is owed at the first dev measurement.

BUDGET: HOLDS

## 16. W6-21 measurements (2026-10-31, emulators only; nothing was run against dzeroth-dev, dzeroth-prod or any cloud project)
Machine: Windows 11 Home, Intel Core i5-1240P (16 logical processors), 16 GB RAM, Go 1.27.0 building the API from `main` 6fdeb8c5 plus #515 (VotePoll, W6-7/8); #520 (ADR-0029 as-built notes) and #521 (GIF delay clamp) were merged into a separate scratch tree only to read and to time the GIF sampler. k6 2.2.0; firebase-tools with Firestore (54080), Auth (54099), Storage (54199), hub (54400), logging (54500) and Pub/Sub on their own port block through a temporary `--config` kept outside the repository and behind `scripts/with-private-tmp.sh` (Pub/Sub refused 54085, 54087 and 54091 with "port not open" or "port taken" and ran on 54093); API on 54081. One local API process ("one instance"). API environment: `ENV=local FEATURE_POSTS=on FEATURE_ENGAGEMENT=on FEATURE_POLLS=on POLL_MIN_DURATION=1m`, defaults for `CACHE_TTL`, and raised for the run only: `RATE_LIMIT_PER_IP_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_PRE_AUTH_IP_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_PER_USER_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_POLL_VOTE_PER_MIN`, `POLL_VOTES_PER_DAY`, `POLL_VOTES_PER_DAY_NEW_ACCOUNT`, `READ_BUDGET_PER_UID_PER_DAY`, `RATE_LIMIT_POST_CREATE_PER_MIN`, `QUOTA_POSTS_PER_DAY`, `QUOTA_NEW_ACCOUNT_POSTS_PER_DAY` (all at 1,000,000 or 100,000). `ENV=local` gives the media module the `AllowAll` moderator, so no Vision call can be made.

Scenario `loadtest/poll_vote.js` (new; the header documents every phase and knob), `make loadtest SCENARIO=poll_vote`, then `node loadtest/analyze_poll.js api.log k6.out`. Setup mints 3,308 users and 25 polls, then idles 70 s so every cache is older than `CACHE_TTL` and the 1-minute poll has closed. One run, 4 min 40 s.

### Cold calls (serial, one virtual user; reads and writes are in the §2 W6 table)
Server time of the request line: new vote 20 to 50 ms, replay 150 ms (first touch of the vote path after the idle), refusals 6 to 11 ms, `GetPost` 4 to 10 ms.

### Hot poll: 3,001 votes at 50 a second on one poll, 60 s, 3,001 distinct voters (the rule-3 shape)
| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| committed (`outcome=voted`) / contention / `txn_attempts` | **3,001 / 0 / 1 on every vote** |
| reads, writes per vote | 3.00 (3 x 2,999, 4 x 2), exactly 3 |
| server time | p50 12 ms, p95 24 ms, p99 64 ms, max 193 ms |
| verify (author `GetPost` after the run) | `total_votes` 3,001 = sum of the four choices (751 / 750 / 750 / 750); nothing lost, nothing double counted |
| WARN / ERROR lines | 0 / 0 |
- **The emulator does not reproduce real Firestore's roughly 1 write a second per document.** This shows the batch is a blind increment with no read inside it (so no read-modify-write loss) and that retries are bounded; it does not forecast contention. The sharding trigger in §6 stands, and the kill switch is `FEATURE_POLLS=off`.
- Typical mix (10 calls a second for 30 s over 20 polls and 200 voters): 150 votes at 3.10 reads and 3 writes, 75 `ALREADY_VOTED` at 1.01 reads (the pool of 200 voters repeats on 20 polls), 75 `GetPost` at 2.07 reads; p95 12 to 17 ms. k6 over the whole run: 9,961 requests, `http_req_duration` avg 13.8 ms, p95 30.4 ms, 0 unexpected responses.

### GIF finalize CPU and memory (a temporary `_test.go` in `internal/media`, not committed)
`GOMAXPROCS=1` to mimic one vCPU, `scanGIF` + `sampleGIF` under the 2 s context, 5 runs each (min / median / max ms), `TotalAlloc` per run, peak `HeapInuse` sampled every 2 ms. Command: `GOWORK=off go test -count=1 -run TestW621GifCPU -v ./internal/media/` (Go 1.27.0, the same core as above, other sessions running on the machine).
| Fixture | bytes | ms min / med / max | TotalAlloc MiB | peak heap MiB |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 64x64 x 10 | 7,696 | 21 / 25 / 30 | 5.1 | 8.9 |
| 320x240 x 30 (typical) | 511,966 | 43 / 45 / 58 | 9.1 | 11.8 |
| 320x240 x 60, delays of 1 cs (the #521 clamp) | 1,024,083 | 121 / 128 / 144 | 11.8 | 14.7 |
| 100x100 x 300 (frame cap) | 818,754 | 103 / 115 / 163 | 13.3 | 15.5 |
| 480x360 x 60 | 2,498,443 | 128 / 137 / 151 | 19.4 | 23.6 |
| 640x480 x 100 | 6,493,378 | 263 / 381 / 569 | 40.0 | 33.2 |
| 800x600 x 40 | 4,073,872 | 411 / 422 / 483 | 29.6 | 26.8 |
| 1280x1280 x 22 (36M px) | 6,850,795 | 489 / 550 / 608 | 46.8 | 40.7 |
| **1280x720 x 54 (49.8M px), 1.7 MiB, within the 2 MiB cap** | 1,728,679 | 629 / 661 / 723 | 50.3 | 27.4 |
| **1280x1280 x 30 (49.2M px), 1.6 MiB, within the 2 MiB cap** | 1,677,885 | 624 / 676 / 798 | 52.3 | 32.2 |
| the same two sizes, hard to compress (9.3 to 9.5 MB, above the upload cap) | 9.3 to 9.5 MB | 598 to 1,266 | 56 to 58 | 50 to 57 |
- **At the area cap (50M pixels, about 13 ns a pixel) a GIF takes 0.63 to 0.80 s, allocates about 51 MiB and holds 27 to 32 MiB live**, which matches ADR-0029 N1 (0.65 s, 51 MiB churn, 26 MiB). Typical GIFs take under 0.15 s. The hard-to-compress fixtures exceed the 2 MiB upload cap and are an upper bound on LZW work only (up to 1.27 s once under load). The 512 MiB instance holds one GIF at a time (the slot), so memory is not a constraint.
- With `AllowAll` the units counted are 2 per GIF (`vision_units=2` on the FinalizeUpload request line in both chain runs).
- **Cost of the CPU:** 0.608 GIFs a DAU-month at about 0.25 s is 0.15 vCPU-s per DAU-month, 0.08% of the 180.5 of the quota table.
- **Against founder decision 1:** 2.5x to 3.2x headroom to the 2 s deadline on this core; whether a Cloud Run vCPU is more than 2.5x slower is the W6-22 smoke's question (§9).

### GCS operations per GIF (counted from `finalize.go`, `gifscreen.go` and `gcs.go`; the Storage emulator has no meter)
| Step | Class A | Class B | Egress |
|---|---|---|---|
| Two signed PUTs (full GIF, thumbnail) | 2 | 0 | in |
| FinalizeUpload: attrs of both objects, 512-byte head of both, the full read (+1 for a GIF), Vision reading the thumbnail by URI | 0 | 2 + 2 + 1 + 1 = 6 | the full GIF, at most 2 MiB (mean 1 MB), us-central1 to asia-south1 |
| Publish: two copies to the public bucket (same region) | 2 | 0 | 0 |
| Deletes of the two upload objects | 0 (free) | 0 | 0 |
| Per GIF | **4** | **6** | **about 1 MB** |
| A play (tap): one GET of the public object | 0 | 1 | about 1 MB to the client |
- Per DAU-month (0.608 GIFs, 9.13 plays): Class A 2.43, Class B 3.65 + 9.13 = 12.8, egress 0.61 + 9.13 = 9.74 MB.
- The thumbnail in a list is a photo-sized object counted in the image-view line already (§1); the full GIF is fetched only on tap.

### Findings (not defects of this ticket)
1. **Resolved by #530 (`post_hash` on the request line and the WARN; §9 now groups by it).** As found: The `VotePoll` request line and the WARN `poll_vote_contention` carry `uid_hash`, `outcome`, `contention` and `txn_attempts` but no post id (checked on the 3,237 request lines of this run). The runbook says to count the WARN per post; it cannot. A backend-developer follow-up: add a short hash of the post id (the `uid_hash` pattern) to both. Until then §9 uses the service-wide vote rate as the necessary condition and a Firestore count by `postId` to find the post.
2. **At the Vision cap GIFs are rejected for every account while photos from established accounts are not** (ADR-0029 F11 point 4): at 1,000 DAU that is the last 8 days of every month (§5). Intended, but a user-visible asymmetry for the founder's ramp plan.
3. **The slot-busy retry has no field of its own.** It is `outcome="rejected:upload_incomplete"` on the request line plus an empty `gif_screen` line, indistinguishable on the request line alone from a genuinely incomplete upload; §9 joins the two.
4. **The mix voters repeat on polls** (25% of mix votes were `ALREADY_VOTED`): the script's pool of 200 voters over 20 polls; it exercises the replay path, not a model of real repeats.

### What was not measured
- **Cloud Run CPU and memory, cold start, instance count, and real Firestore latency and contention**: one local process on emulators. The hot-poll result is behaviour, not a contention forecast; the GIF times are one laptop core, not a Cloud Run vCPU.
- **CreateUpload with a GIF** (no signing credentials on emulators; derived from the photo budget test) and **the Vision call itself** (`AllowAll`; units are counted, not billed). **Eraser and export `poll_votes`** were not re-run here.
- **Every call rate** (0.3 votes, 0.1 closed-poll `GetPost`, 0.02 GIF uploads, 0.3 plays) and **the median GIF size** are inputs with no traffic behind them; the dev allowlist (3 uids) at W6-22 gives the first figures. API response bytes for `VotePoll` (0.8 KB a day) are derived, not measured.
- **A user following 600 accounts, a notification fan-out burst and a cold-start wave**: timeline, push and Cloud Run shapes that polls and GIFs do not touch (a vote emits no notification in ADR-0029). **The shapes that are W6's** (a poll at 50 votes a second, a GIF at the area cap, a typical vote mix) are above.
- **Storage**: GIF objects about 0.63 MB per DAU-month and `pollVotes` about 200 bytes each are derived; the emulator has no storage meter.

Verdict (W6-21): every W6 RPC held its documented read, write and delete budget in the recorded run (the new-vote typical 3.00 to 3.10 against 3, the replay and refusals below the plan, the all-cold vote 5 against the worst 8; GIF finalize writes 3 against 2.7 typical, +11%); the hot poll committed 3,001 of 3,001 votes with no contention on the emulator; an area-cap GIF takes 0.63 to 0.80 s on one core against the 2 s deadline; the Vision cap crossover moves to 730 DAU (plan 747) and GIFs are refused at the cap for every account; W6 costs about INR 53 / 7 / 363 a month at 300 / 1,000 / 3,000 DAU (ADR-0029: 53 / 6 / 342), the Stage 0 bill about INR 544 / 1,803 / 4,852. The reads, already over the free quota before W6, are the approved pay-per-use plan (ADR-0022); writes stay at 61% of the free quota at 300 DAU. The two founder decisions of ADR-0029 N4 are measured by the W6-22 smoke with the §9 queries.

BUDGET: HOLDS

## 17. W5-18 measurements (2026-10-31, emulators only; nothing was run against dzeroth-dev, dzeroth-prod or any cloud project)
Machine: Windows 11 Home, Intel Core i5-1240P (16 logical processors), 16 GB RAM, Go 1.27.0 building the API from `main` f55e6fbc (W5-4..W5-9 merged: lists reads and writes, `GetListTimeline` #524, `SetHomeTabs` #526), k6 2.2.0, firebase-tools with Firestore (59080), Auth (59099), Storage (59199), hub (59400), logging (59500) and Pub/Sub (59093) on the 59xxx block through a temporary `--config` kept outside the repository and behind `scripts/with-private-tmp.sh`; API on 59081. **One local API process is one "instance"** (a fresh process per run). Emulator, API and k6 share the host, there is no network and no Firestore round trip, and **other sessions were using the machine** (the CPU was at 97 to 100% from run 2 on): reads are exact, timings are indicative only.

Scenario `loadtest/list_timeline.js` (new; the header documents every phase and knob), `make loadtest SCENARIO=list_timeline` with `API_URL` and `AUTH_EMULATOR_HOST` set, then `node loadtest/analyze_list_timeline.js api.log k6.out`. Setup: 3 owners, 250 authors with one post each, 12 viewers, 20 herd viewers; three public lists (LA 50 members, LB 150 members = the `LIST_MAX_MEMBERS` default, LC 50 members) with disjoint authors and **different owners**, so an all-cold case is cold in the owner too; four empty lists for the 5-tab case; viewer H follows LC's 50 authors. The serial phase has two further 70 s idles so the tab and list-read groups are cold as well. API environment: `ENV=local FEATURE_LISTS=on FEATURE_POSTS=on FEATURE_GRAPH=on FEATURE_ENGAGEMENT=on` (`FEATURE_REMOVED_IDS=on` in runs 3 and 6), defaults for `CACHE_TTL` (60 s) and `CACHE_LISTS_ENTRIES`, and these at 1,000,000 for the run only: `RATE_LIMIT_PER_IP_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_PRE_AUTH_IP_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_PER_USER_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_LISTS_WRITE_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_LISTS_READ_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_LIST_TIMELINE_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_TIMELINE_PER_MIN`, `RATE_LIMIT_GRAPH_FOLLOW_PER_MIN`, `LIST_MUTATIONS_PER_DAY`, `QUOTA_NEW_ACCOUNT_LIST_ADDS_PER_DAY`, `QUOTA_LIST_ADDS_PER_DAY`, `QUOTA_NEW_ACCOUNT_LIST_CREATES_PER_DAY`, `QUOTA_LIST_CREATES_PER_DAY`, `READ_BUDGET_PER_UID_PER_DAY`, `RATE_LIMIT_POST_CREATE_PER_MIN`, `QUOTA_POSTS_PER_DAY`, `QUOTA_NEW_ACCOUNT_POSTS_PER_DAY`, `QUOTA_FOLLOWS_PER_DAY`, `QUOTA_NEW_ACCOUNT_FOLLOWS_PER_DAY`. `ENV=local` gives the media module the `AllowAll` moderator.

Runs: **1** (first script), **2** and **3** (two idles added; run 3 with `FEATURE_REMOVED_IDS=on` and the old-token step), **4** and **5** (herd only, `COLD_SETTLE=2`, to re-time under load; run 5 with a CPU sampler), **6** (final script, adds the cold `GetMe` with tabs). The serial reads of runs 1, 2, 3 and 6 are identical step for step except where §2 gives two numbers (`SetHomeTabs` 5 on a warm instance in run 1, 14 in run 2, 11 in runs 3 and 6, because a previous step had warmed the lists). Integration tests re-run on the same emulators: `go test -tags=integration ./internal/lists/... ./internal/timeline/... ./internal/identity/...` PASS (lists 12.7 s, timeline 0.6 s, timeline/integration 19.6 s, identity 10.2 s), including the `lists` budget tests (`TestCreateList_Integration_Budget`, `TestAddMember_Integration_Budget` and `_OverflowBudget`, `TestGetList_`, `TestListUserLists_`, `TestListListMembers_Integration_Budget`) and `TestHomeTabsReplace_Integration_Budgets`.

### Reads per call
See the §2 W5 Part A table (every row is a request line of runs 1 to 3 and 6). Headlines: the 150-member list at page 50 reads **106** all cold (worst 111, ceiling 269); 50 members at page 20 reads 46 (worst 48); the empty refresh reads C (2 or 5); a list whose authors Home just read costs **0 to 3**. The ADR-0030 window path (+107, 218 worst) was not reached: with `FEATURE_REMOVED_IDS=on` the old-token refresh cost 11 with `removal_log_reads` 1.

### Server time, warm herd (20 viewers, 8 iterations a second for 40 s = 321 opens at page 20 and 321 refreshes with the `since_token`; one list in four is the 150-member list)
| Run | machine | open (page 20) server p50 / p95 | refresh server p50 / p95 | k6 p95 open / refresh | reads per open / refresh (mean) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | not sampled (the quietest) | 12 / **24** ms | 10 / **26** ms | 25.6 / 26.3 ms | 40.2 / 2.8 |
| 2 | busy | 45 / 100 ms | 43 / 99 ms | 101.7 / 99.7 ms | 40.2 / 2.8 |
| 3 | 97 to 100% CPU | 220 / 568 ms | 219 / 522 ms | 661 / 549 ms | 40.2 / 2.8 |
| 4 | 100% CPU | 438 / 1,801 ms | 417 / 1,652 ms | 1.9 s / 1.67 s | 24.1 / 2.0 |
| 5 | 97% CPU | 183 / 457 ms | 187 / 465 ms | n/a / 480 ms | 23.8 / 1.9 |
| 6 | 100% CPU | not valid: 9 of 436 calls hit the 10 s RPC deadline (`internal`, 23 ERROR lines), p95 about 10 s | | | |
- **Read as: the code path is about 24 to 26 ms at p95 on a quiet machine; the 400 ms target was passed in runs 1 and 2 and missed in runs 3 to 5 only because the host CPU was saturated by other sessions.** The reads (the thing the budget fixes) are identical in every run; the latency moved by 70x with the load. The API process used **5.0 ms of CPU per request** (642 requests in a 39.8 s window of run 5: 3.23 CPU-s; working set 39 to 42 MB), so a Cloud Run vCPU is not the limit at this path. **The warm p95 on dev (W5-19) is the real check; this is not a pass on Cloud Run.** Runs 4 and 5 reuse a warm instance, which is why 100 of 321 opens read 0.
- k6 over run 1: 0 unexpected responses, `http_req_duration` avg 7.95 ms, p95 22.2 ms over the whole run.

### Response sizes behind the egress line (`node loadtest/list_timeline_sizes.js`, body bytes counted on the socket, headers excluded; the API gzips whenever the client sends `Accept-Encoding: gzip`; 40 members with one post of about 90 characters each, pseudo-word text, no images)
| Call | JSON | JSON gzip | binary proto | **proto gzip (the app)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GetListTimeline` page 1, 20 posts | 9,126 | 2,684 | 4,587 | **2,690** |
| `GetListTimeline` refresh | 9,171 | 2,723 | 4,634 | **2,734** |
| `GetListTimeline` page 1, page_size 50 (43 posts exist) | 19,001 | 4,931 | 9,415 | **5,056** |
| `GetList` | 186 | 178 | 97 | **122** |
| `ListUserLists` (1 list) | 189 | 182 | 97 | **122** |
| `ListListMembers` page 20 | 4,251 | 1,135 | 2,144 | **1,070** |
- **The refresh row is a full page, not 3 posts.** The `since_token` watermark is min(request start, `loadedAt` of every author-recent entry used) minus the settle window (`timeline/watermark.go:15`), so on an instance whose entries are younger than the window a refresh re-sends the newest posts (here 20). It is a response-size effect only (0 extra reads) and the client de-duplicates. About 135 bytes a post in proto after gzip.
- **Per DAU per day:** 1.2 x 1.0 KB (a refresh: between 0.5 KB with 3 new posts and 2.7 KB re-sent) + 0.3 x 2.7 + 0.1 x 1.07 + 0.1 x 0.12 + 0.3 x 0.12 + about 0.25 KB of response headers on each of the 2.29 responses (0.57) = **about 2.8 KB**, against the ADR's 18.7 KB. Images are fetched from GCS outside the API and are not measured here (the §5 ceiling carries them).

### Findings (not defects of this ticket; production code was not changed)
1. **Severity Low, config guard.** `LIST_MAX_MEMBERS` has no upper bound: `pkg/platform/config/lists.go:26` loads it with `loadPositiveInts` (`env.go:77-88` only rejects values <= 0), while the per-call read hold is the constant `ReadBudgetMaxCallReads` 269 (`config.go:51`). A Terraform value above about 4,890 would make a page-50 open (6 + C + 100) exceed the hold (the read-budget limiter then refuses the call), and anything above 500 breaks the ADR's stage-ladder trigger (C > 17 per refresh) without an ADR. Suggest a startup check `MaxMembers <= 500`. Not exploitable from a client (the value is operator config, rule 11).
2. **Severity Low, N+1 shape, bounded.** `SetHomeTabs` validates each new tab with a sequential `lists.Get` (`timeline/home_tabs.go:200`, inside `validateNewTabs`), not a batch. Bounded by `HOME_TABS_MAX_PINNED` (5): 11 to 14 reads and 20 to 226 ms (loaded machine) for five foreign lists, 2 to 5 for the typical one new tab. The call is 0.02 per DAU, so the cost is 0.08 reads per DAU. A `GetMany` over the lists would not change the reads (a list is one read either way, plus the per-list owner profile and graph) and is not worth the seam.
3. **Severity Info, doc drift.** The plan's per-RPC table for W5 Part A predates ADR-0030 D7: AddListMember writes 2 (measured 3), RemoveListMember 1 (2), GetList non-owner worst 3 (5), SetHomeTabs worst 7 (19; measured 14), Part A writes 0.51 (0.79). The ADR's as-built table is the correct one and every measured value is inside it; the plan carries a correction note (W5-18).
4. **Severity Info, response size.** A refresh can return a full page of already-seen posts when the author-recent entries are fresh (above). Reads are unaffected; egress is 2.8 KB a day against the ADR's 18.7 KB, so there is nothing to fix.

### What was not measured
- **Cloud Run CPU and memory under real concurrency, cold start, instance count, and real Firestore latency**: one local process on emulators with the host saturated by other sessions after run 1. The warm p95 against 400 ms and the cold start against 1.5 s are the W5-19 dev smoke's questions.
- **The ADR's removal-log window query (+107, 217 to 218 worst)**: not reached (an old-token refresh with `FEATURE_REMOVED_IDS=on` cost 11). It is asserted by the `posts` removals tests and the `ReadBudgetMaxCallReads` pin test (`removed_ids_test.go:32`), not re-run here.
- **`ListUserLists` at 20 lists, a 150-member list with every member posting, a viewer switching 6 tabs a minute**: n = 5 lists was measured and the 20-list worst derived; with every member posting the reads are still capped at C x k <= 100 + 6 (the chunk limit, not the number of posts); a tab switch is one refresh (2 to 11 reads), and the 12 a minute per instance bucket bounds it. The herd (8 refreshes a second over 20 viewers) covers the rate.
- **Every call rate** (1.2 refreshes, 0.3 cold opens, the 30% pinned-list share, 40 members) is an input with no traffic behind it; the dev allowlist of W5-19 gives the first figures. API response bytes are measured with no images, and **list image egress** (the ADR's +10% ceiling) is not measured.
- **A user following 600 accounts, a notification fan-out burst and a cold-start wave**: Home, push and Cloud Run shapes that Part A does not touch (adds and removes emit no notification, ADR-0030 non-goals). The Part A shapes (a 150-member list at page 50, a refresh with 0 and 5 new, five tabs cold, the herd, and the author-recent entries shared with Home) are above.

Verdict (W5-18): every Part A RPC held its documented read and write budget in the recorded runs (worst measured: 106 reads for the 150-member page 50 against the ADR's 111 and the 269 hold; 46 for 50 members against 48; `SetHomeTabs` 14 against 19); the aggregate is 24.35 reads, 0.79 writes, 0.29 deletes and 2.29 requests per DAU a day (plan +10% on reads, ADR +9%); the free reads cross at 130 DAU (138 before), writes at 484 (493), writes at 300 DAU stay at 62% of the free quota and 78% of the 80% line; Part A costs about INR 8 / 44 / 140 a month at 300 / 1,000 / 3,000 DAU (ADR: 9 / 39 / 150) and the Stage 0 bill is about INR 552 / 1,847 / 4,992. The reads, already over the free quota before Part A, are the approved pay-per-use plan (ADR-0022). The list-feed trigger is at 5.1% of reads against 15%. Latency is not proven here: 24 to 26 ms at p95 on a quiet host, unusable numbers on the saturated one; the dev smoke of W5-19 owns that check.

BUDGET: HOLDS
